## Native gRPC dependencies matched to upstream - 2026-10-15

The paired managed Grpc.Core dependency contains version2.46.6. The exact grpc.core2.46.6 package was fetched from NuGet's flat-container endpoint and SHA256-pinned (ffc1e3222ef60cc347f163021dc8a71263707475ae75995cdececade1115ded3). Both previously UPX-unpacked native gRPC DLLs have byte-identical executable .text sections and ordinary data/resources compared with that package. Section layouts/flags and entry points agree; all208 x64 and204 x86 import bindings agree at their IAT addresses.

The394 x64 and385 x86 changed section bytes are confined to debug records and import descriptors/name hints/DLL-name casing. The security directory and IAT directory entries are cleared in the unpacked files. Full file hashes therefore differ; no exact-file identity or signature preservation is claimed. The comparison initially rejected unaccounted import hints and case-only DLL names; those fields were explicitly parsed and bindings verified rather than broadening the allowance to entire sections. There are no unexplained section-byte differences. Evidence: artifacts/il-review/grpc-native-upstream-sections.json, grpc-native-upstream-proof.json and verify-grpc-native.py; references under C:/cilantro-verify/reference-grpc-2.46.6. Corpus binaries were not executed.

This establishes the observed gRPC native dependencies as upstream library code with UPX packaging changes, not a demonstrated remaining Reactor reconstruction gap. The two VGeko native libraries are separately identified as Go1.19.5 application code. These classifications do not claim to remove arbitrary native protections beyond the .NET Reactor objective. Existing extraction/unpacking provenance is retained. IDA x64 gRPC autoanalysis returned cancellation before completion (instance3f0433ecd967); no completed IDA analysis is claimed for it. The upstream byte/import comparisons supply the evidence independently.

## Final targeted reference mismatch repaired - 2026-10-15

ReferenceLookupTable now identifies the private readonly enum lookup array by exact initialized bytes, enum type and module binding. It requires a single assignment reached along the static constructor's unconditional path, rejects alternate entry into the initialization sequence, rejects extra writes/address uses, and limits every read to the independently matched ValidateToken consumer. Raw initializer data must belong to the same module and have only RuntimeHelpers.InitializeArray token uses. The key contains data and type evidence, not a sample hash or generated field name. ## Raw payload-buffer proof and static startup replay - 2026-10-15

RawAssemblyBufferUse now checks every use of a local byte array via evaluation-stack origins. Only exact core-library Assembly.Load first arguments and full Stream.Read buffers/counts are accepted; length results cannot escape to other computations, and aliases, local addresses, extra readers, duplicate consumers and foreign framework lookalikes refuse. This is a consumer proof, not yet proof of allocation/resource-stream provenance. Seven synthetic controls pass. Applied to the actual wrapper, payload buffer local1 passes; optional symbols buffer local2 refuses because it has additional initialization/uses. That optional path must be resolved before automatic integration, rather than exempted by sample name.

## Unsupported wrapper narrowed to packaging and startup handoff - 2026-10-15

Complete metadata/IL inventory of5bbe identifies an IL-only DLL with no managed/native entry point, one ordinary type with five methods, and one embedded resource. The resource bytes hash exactly to83ba5d833eba38f578eb8478f8961a0bddb63ff35016b40d8e0536b164ee1ed3, the successfully recovered Reactor stage: no resource decoding or missing embedded stage remains in this wrapper. The public startup method reads that resource, loads it, registers assembly resolution, supplies WPF ResourceAssembly support, forwards command-line arguments and invokes the child entry point. Other methods are resolution callbacks and constructors. The bounded interpreter stops at the explicit captured-child MethodBase.Invoke boundary; this is not evidence of a hidden VM or failed payload decoding.

The root still has no cleaned output and remains unsuccessful. Its original resource still contains the protected child; separately recovering the child does not replace that resource. The remaining work is to support a verified managed-bootstrap handoff/reassembly that preserves the wrapper API and behavior, with honest report semantics. Do not simply waive protector-identity, mark arbitrary unknown containers successful, or delete the wrapper on the assumption that its exported startup API is unused. Complete method inventory and exact resource/payload/child links are saved in artifacts/il-review/unsupported-container-inventory.json and unsupported-container-diagnosis.json. This narrows the gap; it does not close it.

## Reflection subclass scope and unsupported-container diagnosis - 2026-10-15

The four remaining JToken relationship consumers need upstream provenance. The generatedMethod_3766 predicate obtains its receiver through GetMemberUnderlyingType, ImplementsGenericDefinition's out-Type result, and GetGenericArguments; its local Type value is not directly grounded by the existing Object.GetType/ldtoken rule. Its exact body is saved in artifacts/il-review/jtoken-predicate-body.jsonl.

The pinned-reference hierarchy audit now additionally inventories System.Type, MemberInfo, FieldInfo, PropertyInfo and MethodInfo subclasses across the ten native-chain assemblies. ## JSON receiver and alias flow - 2026-10-15

JsonReceiverFlow now combines default-instance provenance, cross-block stack producers, closed call-site storage and decoded binders. It models a restricted framework graph, enumeration, dictionary reads, positional dynamic indexing/member calls, scalar conversions and Boolean operations. Graph addresses, field/array stores, unproved calls, unknown replacement receivers and collection mutation are refused. ## Exact CSharp binder argument decoding - 2026-10-15

CSharpBinderArguments decodes exact framework Convert, GetIndex, InvokeMember, UnaryOperation and BinaryOperation calls. Argument-info arrays must be allocated and fully initialized in the binder's basic block, with one constant-index store per entry and no other escape or write. ### Erased callback parameter census identifies the next reconstruction family

A static census of the latest saved native managed output (`type-identity-array/000.dll`) finds 173 adjacent function-pointer/delegate constructions targeting 161 methods with Object parameters. Sixteen distinct target methods correspond to sixteen of the 204 remaining IL findings. These include JsonDynamicContract factories, the StringsHeap comparison, HTTP header parsers and validators, HttpClient content callbacks, and an HttpClientHandler predicate. This is an opportunity census, not sixteen proved-safe repairs or proof that all other findings are legitimate.

The existing closed-caller typing pass deliberately excludes function-pointer targets. Delegate construction alone cannot replace that gate: delegates can escape, expose their Method, or be invoked through reflection. The inspected CacheControlHeaderValue callback flows through a private static field into a generic Collection<T> helper and then a private instance field before Action<T>.Invoke. A repair needs a closed invocation-use proof covering these transfers, generic substitutions, every target reference and reflection exposure; it must reject unknown escapes. Other candidates include framework consumers such as Enumerable.Where and List sorting, requiring explicit consumer contracts rather than a blanket delegate assumption.

Evidence: `artifacts/il-review/native-callback-census.jsonl` records each construction, target body and all CIL method references; `native-callback-summary.json` correlates targets with the existing IL report. The diagnostic built successfully and only parsed sample metadata; no sample code was executed and no recovered output was changed. The census is limited to adjacent ldftn/newobj sequences (ignoring nops), so it is not exhaustive delegate analysis. The two original-input runners were revalidated as live processes during this investigation; neither had a completed root outcome yet. Full unprotection remains open.

### Type-identity-only use unblocks a remaining array reconstruction repair

A local helper receives typeof(UTF8String) solely for a ceq comparison. ## Frozen release baseline

The following baseline was measured **2026-10-12 (America/Toronto)** using release commit `dd43b75803f822fcc957cdc81bf9a9f6f6fc67dd` (0.10.5). Its counts remain unchanged for comparison with the later checkpoints.

**Full removal of all Reactor protections is not yet achieved.** VM removal is reconfirmed for every intact VM-bearing input. The principal remaining work is obsolete protector code and resources, completion-gate verification debt, and a final full-scope recovery sweep. Targeted original-input trials now also complete the identified 1,222-call string-cleanup item while preserving shared decoder initialization.

**Correction to the preceding report:** its corpus-wide opaque-guard closure was too broad. The native-stage guard removal remains verified, but the frozen 0.10.5 inspection found **5,516 remaining integer-pool reads in mc.dll**. The preceding final artifact had the same reads across the same 1,277 methods: this was missed coverage, not a new regression. The fresh post-release trial removes them; the frozen sweep below remains the unchanged comparison baseline.

The frozen corpus contains 85 baseline assembly identities. This run covers **all 84 intact baseline identities**, excluding only the previously accepted damaged NETReactorSlayer Lqcuzgc rewrite (`c76290907b62…`). Its intact original (`81cf796c987d…`) is included. The four additional identities are VGeko's newly extracted native DLLs.

The Vault inventory remains the previously frozen **29 `protector:reactor` hashes**, not a new query. All **28 undamaged tagged roots** are included, alongside the pinned probes, unprotected controls and ConfuserEx controls.

The seven incomplete root results are the **three intentional unprotected controls**, plus **VGeko, the already unwrapped WindowsManagement managed wrapper, Gui.exe, and the historical deobfuscated logger**, where the ordinary policy recognizes no supported protector. They are not seven failed Reactor VM recoveries. Root verification alone does not imply that a cleaned file was emitted.

Separately labeled generic-mode checks emit verified output for all four non-control inputs. The logger also decompiles with ILSpy exit 0. VGeko has **13 recovered managed nodes and four native extracted-only nodes**; its overall chain remains incomplete. The GUI and managed-wrapper follow-ups are root-only checks; recursive child coverage comes from the ordinary sweep. These follow-ups do not overwrite the ordinary-policy matrix.

Recovery used the same declarations, host profiles and standard **192-million-step budget** as the preceding baseline, with reconstruction and recursive recovery enabled. Corpus assertions were not substituted for measurement. All recovered children with analysis verify; the four native DLLs have extraction status rather than managed-verification claims. The longest emitted path is 277 characters.

No malware was executed. These trials used bounded static interpretation, dnlib metadata inspection and static decompilation.

## What is confirmed closed

**VM machinery removal:** independent physical-removal checks pass in **18 recovery contexts covering all 14 intact VM-bearing input hashes**. No identified VM type remains. All **28 reconstructed methods/programs** survive as CIL across the unique inputs, and the **14 identified VM resources** are removed. There are no unread VM operations reported in the fresh sweep. The damaged rewrite remains outside the repair obligation; it is not counted as successfully unprotected.

**Native guard removal:** the native managed stage has no remaining identified Reactor integer guard pool. All three entry paths—native bootstrap, already unwrapped managed wrapper, and direct managed-stage input—produce the same cleaned-stage hash, `cad3780ddbc03b26876873db0c3d9df43535a7590cdbc886c45c9be9280ab6b8`. Its **442 previously reviewed application switches** remain, with unchanged per-method token counts. Repeating guard completion and control-flow finalization makes no further changes. The coupled-startup evidence records matching captured application state and native writes; the raw VM-state disagreement remains visible.

**Payload and path coverage:** every payload hash captured in the preceding baseline is captured again. The sweep adds the four VGeko native DLLs and encounters no historical Windows-path reload failures.

The earlier post-recovery review classified **933 application switches across 83 managed outputs**. That remains historical selector evidence, not a new claim that all protector conditionals are absent from fresh outputs. mc.dll's remaining field-based guards demonstrate why a switch-only census was insufficient.

## Remaining work, in priority order

### 1. Remove mc.dll's non-virtualized opaque guards — resolved after 0.10.5

The frozen `mc.dll` (`17fa9508bf92…`) retained **5,516 integer-field reads across 1,277 methods** from the original Reactor pool `<Module>{f24410e4-b55b-41b6-88bc-2ff5e4fd3988}`, emitted as `GeneratedType_0075`. The type had **126 integer fields and two other fields**. Surviving application CIL included branches directly controlled by those fields.

The completion proof now also recognizes ordinary Reactor module/GUID pools with a complete hashed integer-field family. It proves the pool's null-state helpers, folds 39 same-owner calls inside its initializer, and then requires the same closed construction/use proof as VM-reconstructed pools. General reflective-write safety remains unchanged. Exposed handles, names, addresses, writers, unknown initializer effects and escaped pool objects still refuse the transformation.

A fresh original-input recursive run removes **all 5,516 reads and all 126 integer fields**, with zero method rollbacks. Output instructions fall from **120,141 to 112,907**. All **94 application switches** retain their method/target-count inventory, all **3,044 existing method bodies** retain their exception-handler kind inventory, structural verification passes, and the recovered child assembly remains byte-identical. That first fix retained the initialization/null-check shim; the subsequent narrowly proved removal is recorded in item 4.

All **28 focused tests** pass, including ordinary and reconstructed initializers, initialization exceptions, recursive entry, application switches and refusal/rollback cases. A replay of the changed pass over all **76 distinct emitted managed inputs** in the frozen sweep changes only mc.dll, with all 76 structural checks passing and no switch-count differences. This is a pass-level regression replay, not a second full recovery sweep. The broader fast suite passes 1,342 tests; 14 fail because their sample files are absent from the checkout.

Evidence: `artifacts/ordinary-reactor-guards/comparison.json`, `emitted-audit.jsonl`, `corpus-pass-replay.jsonl` and `test-results/`. ### 2. Remove reachable Reactor self-integrity checks — resolved after 0.10.5

The frozen **Lqcuzgc** (`81cf796c987d…`) and **Uescfpvjk** (`aa553a78dbe0…`) each retained a self-integrity routine reached from **82 IL call sites**, including the module initializer. The routines contained `RSACryptoServiceProvider.VerifyHash` and a `" is tampered."` exception path.

Both originals reject their on-disk file signature; recovery already records that refusal and follows the protector's own memory-load path. The new completion pass specializes **only the recorded self-integrity routine's own-assembly location** to that memory-load case. Application location queries remain unchanged. It preserves receiver evaluation, crypto-cache initialization and other shared state, compares two independent cold startups before and after, and removes exception regions only when none can be entered. This intentionally preserves the recovered memory-loaded payload's behavior rather than reinstating the original file rejection in the cleaned assembly.

Fresh original-input runs both succeed and verify. Lqcuzgc's routine shrinks **2,196 → 48 instructions**; Uescfpvjk's shrinks **2,214 → 50**. Neither retains a live signature check or tamper exception. All **8 and 9 application switches**, respectively, retain their method/target-count inventories; reconstructed bodies remain present and no identified VM type returns. **57 focused tests** pass. The fast suite has 1,353 passes and the same 14 missing-sample failures.

The selector census covers all 76 distinct frozen managed outputs. Besides these two routines, it identifies only the already-unused native-stage routine. That routine and the unused reconstructed copies still belong to obsolete-code cleanup in item 4. The remaining initialization shims are not claimed to be complete protector-type removal.

Evidence: `artifacts/integrity-completion/comparison.json`, `emitted-audit.jsonl`, `corpus-selectors.jsonl`, `fresh-runs.json` and `test-results/`. Fresh original-input outputs are under `C:/cilantro-verify/review-trials/memory-integrity-final/`.

### 3. Remove residual string-table machinery after literal recovery

The frozen baseline has eight payloads with **1,222 calls** whose returned string is discarded before a recovered literal is loaded. **All 1,222 are now removed in the recorded targeted original-input trials**, with the complete eight-case replay also passing. Required shared initialization is preserved explicitly; this is not permission to delete the decoder still used by embedded-assembly loading.

All eight target helpers read an AppDomain string table. The strings are already readable; the calls preserve initializer/domain effects. Establish ownership and surviving consumers of that state, then remove or replace the redundant machinery without deleting application-observable behavior. Keep attribution of this additional string layer explicit.

The follow-up census finds exactly one direct `AppDomain.GetData` site and one `SetData` site in each of the eight emitted assemblies, both belonging to this string layer. This narrows the next ownership proof but does not by itself rule out reflective or external consumers. Evidence: `artifacts/integrity-completion/domain-slot-consumers.json`.

A conservative completion pass now handles a fully owned string table and its exclusively referenced private dependencies. It requires every surviving call to match its previously recovered literal, refuses exposed names/handles, additional domain consumers and escaping table/metadata references, and compares independent startup snapshots on both sides. Only the specifically owned domain slot is omitted; other static, domain, network, native and captured-payload state remains in the comparison. Shared helper state is not exempted. Twelve focused ownership/rollback tests pass, including exclusive versus shared decoder helpers.

The broader selected suite passes **127 tests**, including the original Lqcuzgc string-recovery test. The fast suite passes **1,366 tests**, with the same **14 missing-sample failures**; the domain-snapshot regression confirms that excluding the owned slot still detects other slot changes and table aliases escaping into application state.

### 4. Delete attributable, obsolete protector code and data

The frozen outputs rooted every reconstructed method. **27 of the 28 reconstructed bodies** are otherwise not ordinarily reachable, externally visible or explicitly reflectively exposed under the tool's analysis. The native singleton initializer is the exception. Cleanup now admits a lifted body when its recorded original caller was positively orphaned; ordinary reachability, exposure and surviving-reference checks still apply, and unrelated reconstructed application methods remain roots. New methods are tracked by object identity rather than their shared unassigned token, with separate added-method accounting. The live Lqcuzgc audit found its pool initializer's caller was **not** orphaned, so that rule alone correctly left it in place.

The frozen sweep retained **21 generated integer-pool types containing 2,462 total fields**. mc.dll accounts for the active pool above. The other **20 pools, containing 2,334 fields**, have no direct field reads from outside their pool types. Review all references and initializer effects before removing the complete types. The small native initialization helper still preserves effects under the current proof; it must not be deleted solely because its name mentions the protector.

**Post-release pool-removal progress:** the closed Reactor-pool proof previously required a remaining read, excluding wholly unused pools. It now also identifies zero-read pools for a separate removal pass, which requires no references from any surviving declaration and retains the existing construction, naming, handle, writer and numeric-token checks. Removal records identify reconstructed initializer bodies and their fingerprints. Replaying this pass over all **76 distinct frozen managed outputs** removes **13 pools, 1,522 fields, 78 methods and 16,509 IL instructions**, including 13 reconstructed initializers. All surviving method bodies remain structurally identical; resources remain unchanged; all 76 structural checks and serialized reload checks pass, with zero writer complaints. This is a pass replay, not full-corpus original-input closure. Evidence: `artifacts/orphaned-rebuilt/pool-replay-verified.jsonl` and `pool-replay-summary.json`.

The ordinary initializer completion proof likewise now accepts zero-read pools, so its null guards do not prevent subsequent whole-pool removal. Fresh original-input runs remove four further ordinary pools in Ggdzylx, Frzfqnvunjz, Ktjtiuwiixd and Msfhjjpkive: **460 fields and 12 methods**. Together with the independent replay, that establishes removal for **17 distinct pool-bearing inputs**, covering **1,982 fields and 90 methods**. These totals combine a pass replay and targeted fresh runs; they do not substitute for the final whole-corpus recovery sweep.

The fresh Lqcuzgc run confirms removal of its pool, 112 fields and six methods, including the obsolete reconstructed initializer. ### 5. Bring completion gates into line with the actual contract


The independent physical VM-removal gate passes all 18 baseline contexts. The recorded older `corpus/verify-reconstruction.ps1` gate fails for both Qbjuef and Lqcuzgc because it still requires full repeat-call state agreement; Lqcuzgc also failed its 564-type threshold at that checkpoint. The recorded repeat-call differences concern VM state, which the accepted contract permits removing. Retain those raw diagnostics, but update closure checks to require application/payload preservation and physical removal rather than agreement over deliberately removed VM machinery. The separate three-type string-helper remainder has since been removed in the targeted trials above; the gate needs a fresh run against the final outputs.

When an otherwise unused reconstructed body is positively identified as protector-only, the gate should accept its recorded removal while still requiring every application body and payload. Keeping all 28 reconstructed bodies forever is not itself the user's completion criterion.

Keep a per-input inventory of live protection calls, retained protector declarations and data, expected payloads and explicit exclusions. Pipeline `Success`, structural verification, a detector confidence score, or zero unresolved detected strings must not be used as a full-unprotection verdict.

**Additional verification debt exposed by the post-release test run:** the default-run Lqcuzgc IL-verifier test expects `(input findings, output findings, newly failing methods, worsened methods) = (377, 266, 1, 0)` but reports `(377, 764, 49, 17)`. Repeating that test with the frozen 0.10.5 core reproduces the identical discrepancy, so the ordinary-pool fix did not introduce it. This default-policy test is distinct from the explicitly configured recursive corpus sweep; investigate the differing options/artifacts and findings before updating any expectation. The observed failure must not be hidden by the structural-verification results above.

**The IL-verifier investigation now has a concrete accessibility lead.** Running the existing test verifier directly against the original Lqcuzgc and the saved `late-loader/81cf796c987d/cleaned.dll` gives 377 and 706 findings respectively. Of the latter, **613 are `FieldAccess`**, 82 are `StackUnexpected` and 11 are `ExpectedArray`. A representative newly visible access is an assembly-visible singleton field on a private nested helper type, read from an unrelated application type. This is not resolved by a structural-verifier pass. The transformation responsible and the legal way to preserve its initialization effects still need to be established; no visibility widening or expectation update has been made. This saved output uses different options from the default regression test, so these counts do not replace its 764-finding result. Evidence: `artifacts/il-review/lqc-readings.jsonl`, `summary.json` (including exact file hashes), and `lqc-type.il.txt`; the diagnostic invokes the existing test verifier and does not execute sample code.

**The numeric-accessibility defect is fixed and the default regression expectation is refreshed from evidence.** A fresh run using the regression test's exact recovery options (preserve tokens, no renaming, no declarations) now emits successfully. The same IL verifier reports **377 input findings, 101 output findings, nine newly reported methods and zero worsened methods**; all nine new methods were unreadable in the protected input. Output findings are 89 `StackUnexpected` and 12 `ExpectedArray`, with **zero `FieldAccess`**. Independent serialization/reload and structural checks pass with no writer errors. The old 764-finding discrepancy is therefore resolved; the remaining 101 findings still require analysis for the full-unprotection goal. ## Other findings and scope boundaries

**Numeric recovery accessibility implementation.** The representative Lqcuzgc access was introduced by `numeric-tables-completion`: replacing a table lookup with a constant copied the holder read into an unrelated caller. Recovery now keeps inaccessible holder reads inside an assembly-visible void helper beside the original getter, preserving both the getter-owner and holder initialization triggers. It does not widen existing type or field visibility. New helpers are declared separately to the structural rewrite allowance, including across the earlier virtualization pass. The first default-options empirical trial correctly refused emission because helper additions were not yet declared; that accounting defect was fixed before the successful checked trial above. Evidence: `artifacts/il-review/numeric-bridge-allowance-tests.log`.

- **One stub-shaped method is not a demonstrated missing body.** mc.dll's remaining diagnostic identifies `MC.EQL.Data.ListDecorator/EmptyList::Count()`, whose CIL returns 64-bit zero. The recorded initializer never patched that target, while restoring the other 5,366 catalogued bodies. Its empty-list role and inspected body explain the shape; it is not counted here as confirmed lost application code.
- **Extraction continuation is still bounded.** Captured payloads are preserved, but some paths stop after capture at denied `Assembly.GetType` / `GetReferencedAssemblies` operations, or at unknown protobuf, registry or awaited-task state. Separate these evidence limits from actual protection remaining in emitted CIL. Removing Reactor protection does not require executing every later malware behavior or fetching remote content.
- **Generic-mode inputs are a policy distinction.** The plain wrappers and historical logger can emit verified output with explicit generic recovery. The three unprotected controls are expected negative detections.
- **Native dependency deprotection is separate.** VGeko's four native DLLs are extracted. Their earlier optional UPX-unpacking evidence remains available, but neither extraction nor unpacking establishes arbitrary native deprotection, and that is not automatically a remaining .NET Reactor mechanism.
- **Damaged-input repair is excluded.** The NETReactorSlayer rewrite remains unsupported; its intact original is handled. Arbitrary damaged-metadata repair, original symbol recovery and warning-free decompiler source are not additional requirements silently imposed on this corpus goal.

### Resource-access blockers narrowed

Resource cleanup now recognizes the closed framework expression `GetExecutingAssembly → GetName → GetPublicKey`, including exact framework casts and the existing initialization-only padding. The assembly objects cannot escape that expression; alternate entries, escaping values and foreign lookalikes remain blockers. The calls are not changed. The exact zero-argument `kernel32.dll!IsDebuggerPresent` Boolean/int32 import is also recognized as non-resource access, following its [documented Windows contract](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/debugapi/nf-debugapi-isdebuggerpresent); the import and its behavior remain intact.

A diagnostic replay against the saved Lqcuzgc output reduces the named resource-access blockers from five to three. It still removes nothing. Remaining are the application's dynamic assembly invocation method and two methods of the Reactor embedded-assembly resolver. Static decompilation shows the resolver retains bundle lookup, decompression, assembly caching, and both `Assembly.Load(byte[])` and temporary-file `Assembly.LoadFile` paths. This is substantive surviving protector functionality: replacing the resolver must preserve dependency selection, identity/cache behavior and loading mode. The common decoder remains needed while that consumer survives.

Evidence: `artifacts/il-review/resource-access-lqc.json` and `resource-access-resolver.cs`. The replay supplies the independently attributed `CTdD` identity solely to exercise the access gate, emits no assembly, and is not a fresh pipeline/corpus result. All **39 focused tests** pass; the fast suite has **1,588 passes and the established 14 missing-sample failures**. No further resource removal is claimed.

### Encrypted dependency bundle decoded

Static inspection of Lqcuzgc's retained resolver established the `wuJlj` bundle index and its loading-mode flag. The bundle contains one dependency: `protobuf-net, Version=2.4.0.0, Culture=neutral, PublicKeyToken=257b51d87d2e4d67`, configured for in-memory `Assembly.Load(byte[])` (mode 0). Its encoded slice is 183,523 bytes at offset 8. The initial decode exhausted the ordinary 192-million-step budget in the managed decompressor; two independent decodes completed at **201,901,889 steps each**, producing identical **288,256-byte** managed assemblies with the expected identity.

The exact captured SHA-256 is `b2a66e9864f81c2800a5afef4bfd1faf7c910e5a43ea2eb9dbb15c8ab6ffc633`. This differs from the existing local `protobuf-net.dll` reference copy; that library must not be substituted for the bundled bytes. A further pair of bounded 256-million-step decodes reproduced the result, and the capture was saved under `C:/cilantro-verify/review-trials/dependency-capture/lqc/` using its hash as the filename. Static IL verification reads every method and reports four findings; this is not a zero-findings claim.

This dependency hash is absent from the frozen scope/observation records checked here. Track it as an additional discovered dependency in the next full-scope audit, without rewriting the historical baseline. The production pipeline does **not yet** replace the encrypted dependency resolver or emit this capture automatically. Remaining implementation must preserve its exact bytes, identity matching/fallback, cache behavior and loading mode, then remove the now-unused bundle reader and decompressor. The diagnostic uses the format constants established from this resolver; generic format discovery and refusal tests remain part of integration.

Evidence: `artifacts/il-review/dependency-probe-lqc{,-high}.jsonl`, `dependency-capture-lqc.jsonl`, and `dependency-capture-ilverify.jsonl`. The diagnostic project now explicitly compiles only `Program.cs`, excluding decompiler output from its build inputs. No recovered assembly was executed.

### Dependency capture integrated into the pipeline

`EmbeddedDependencyRecoveryPass` now discovers static resource-backed assembly resolvers by framework call structure, queries declared assembly references, and requires two agreeing cold responses. Captures must be valid managed assemblies with matching dependency names and one exact unchanged source resource. They join the normal extracted artifacts, hashes and reports. There are no sample-specific method names, bundle constants or dependency names in this pass. It has limits of four candidate resolvers, 64 request names and a shared 768-million-step budget, subject to declared budget overrides. This recovers referenced entries; it does not establish coverage of unreferenced bundle entries or replace the resolver.

A fresh Lqcuzgc original-input run succeeds and verifies under `C:/cilantro-verify/review-trials/dependency-recovery/lqc/`. The pass spends 403,838,416 interpreted steps (41.7 seconds in this trial), emits the exact 288,256-byte `protobuf-net` dependency, and records its written path with origin `embeddedDependency`. The on-disk hash matches the independent capture. The cleaned parent assembly is byte-identical to the preceding output. This trial uses root recovery; it is not a recursive-child completion claim.

Historical clarification: `docs/completion-progress.md` already records successful controlled resolver probes yielding this same dependency hash under `artifacts/resolver-decompressor/`. The recent decoding reconfirmed that evidence on current output. The new result here is automatic production extraction/reporting, not first discovery of the dependency. The frozen full-scope observation records still need to be supplemented in the next corpus audit.

The same stage also succeeds on saved Uescfpvjk output without configuration changes: two responses from its differently named `AXtb` bundle recover the identical dependency hash in 384,356,957 total steps. This is a saved-output replay, not another original-input pipeline trial. Evidence: `artifacts/il-review/dependency-recovery-uesc-replay.json`.

All five new tests pass, covering successful capture/deduplication, foreign framework lookalikes, wrong assembly identity, exceptions and budget exhaustion. ### Dependency lookup-table request discovery

Before a resolver can be replaced, extraction must account for dependencies that have no `AssemblyRef` row. Both requests must finish with null responses, no captured assembly loads and identical string key sets. Those names extend the bounded candidate request set; every captured dependency still requires the existing two-response, identity and source-resource validation. Differing table snapshots are refused as a discovery source. Snapshot agreement alone does not authorize resolver deletion or prove every possible table state.

Saved-output replays for Lqcuzgc and Uescfpvjk each discover one table name and recover the same exact `protobuf-net` dependency. These are stage replays, not fresh whole-pipeline runs. Seven focused tests pass, including an assembly absent from the reference metadata and refusal of a query-dependent table. The fast suite has **1,595 passes and the established 14 missing-sample failures**. ### Empty instruction scaffolding removed

The final pipeline now compacts no-ops and unconditional branches to the next retained instruction. Branch and switch targets are redirected without changing switch entries. Exception boundaries and their predecessors, sequence points, conditional stack-consuming branches, `leave` instructions, self-loops and terminal padding are retained. Methods are edited transactionally and refuse increased stack-analysis diagnostics. This removes instruction scaffolding; it does not itself replace the encrypted dependency resolver.

The saved-output replay covers **96 contexts representing 76 distinct input hashes**. All serialize/reload and pass structural verification; **57 contexts change**, removing **1,927,432 empty instructions across contexts**. That aggregate includes repeated inputs and is not a unique-input total or a fresh full-corpus recovery sweep.

Fresh Lqcuzgc recovery succeeds and verifies, removing **17,158 instructions from 551 methods**: **37,801 → 20,643 instructions**, with type/method counts unchanged. All seven surviving switch method/target-count entries and all resource hashes are preserved. The full per-method IL-verifier finding map is unchanged at 47 findings. The emitted `protobuf-net` dependency hash still matches the independently captured bytes. Replaying dependency recovery on the compacted assembly again produces that exact payload and reduces total interpreted work from **403,841,859 to 250,443,991 steps** (about 38% less). This replay validates that dependency path, not arbitrary application execution.

Four focused compaction tests cover switch outcomes/duplicate entries, fall-through chains, conditional stack consumption, self-loops, `leave`, and exception boundaries. The fast suite has **1,599 passes and the established 14 missing-sample failures**. Evidence: `artifacts/il-review/compaction-replay{,-summary}.json[l]`, `compaction-lqc-{summary,audit,reading,manifest,dependency}.json[l]`, and the matching test log/TRX. Fresh output: `C:/cilantro-verify/review-trials/il-compaction/lqc/`. Encrypted dependency resolver replacement and decoder/resource retirement remain open.

### Array and copied-field type erasure reduced

Field restoration now recognizes array allocations, concretely typed locals and copies from other fields. Proven types propagate through copy chains before metadata changes are committed. Every writer must agree, including assembly identity; matching type names from different assemblies are insufficient. Ambiguous object locals, exposed field addresses, conflicting stores and alternate control-flow entries into stores are refused. Instructions, initialization triggers and exception behavior are not rewritten by this pass.

The same **96 saved contexts** now yield **221 restored fields in 26 contexts**, **120 more fields** than the preceding field-restoration replay. All pass and serialized structural checks succeed. Counts include repeated inputs; this is a saved-output replay, not a fresh whole-corpus run or whole-corpus IL-verifier result.

Against the protected input's 377 findings, the six newly reported methods were all previously unreadable, and no previously readable method worsens. The pinned corpus expectation now records that comparison. Final type/method/instruction counts, all seven switch records and all resource hashes are unchanged. Seven new cases cover array/typed-local copies, field-copy propagation, ambiguous locals, conflicting writes, branch bypasses, exposed addresses and distinct assembly identities. ### Fixed-resource dependency index construction established

`DependencyIndexConstruction` now checks the index-building body and its fresh record constructors before interpreting it. Every path to a table read or normal return must pass the unique fresh Hashtable publication. The permitted calls are scoped framework stream/reader, string, Hashtable and object-construction operations; ambient reads, additional static state, callbacks, field addresses, unsupported constructors and finalizers refuse the proof. Two isolated executions must agree on entry names, assigned integer record fields and final stream position. The result carries the exact resource name and hash.

This establishes construction for a **supplied fixed byte stream**. It does not establish that every caller supplies that stream, that the table cannot subsequently be changed, or that concurrent calls are correctly serialized. Those caller/consumer obligations remain prerequisites for physical decoder replacement. Real declaring-type initialization is excluded from this body evaluation and must remain on the runtime path.

The proof passes on both remaining dependency bundles: Lqcuzgc `wuJlj` (183,790 bytes) and Uescfpvjk `AXtb` (183,806 bytes). Each constructs the protobuf-net 2.4.0.0 entry with offset **8** and loading mode **0**. These values are obtained from the actual index constructors, without hardcoded format masks or entry offsets. `EmbeddedDependencyRecoveryPass` now retains the proved snapshots as `dependency.validatedIndexes` after source-backed recovery. Its Lqcuzgc replay still captures the exact same protobuf-net bytes. This turn does not modify emitted assemblies or claim a new decoder/resource deletion.

Seven construction tests cover valid input, ambient state, reads of the old table, bypassed initialization, foreign framework lookalikes, finalizers and damaged bytes. All **14 focused dependency tests pass**. The fast suite has **1,613 passes and the established 14 missing-sample failures**. Evidence: `artifacts/il-review/index-construction-{lqc,uesc}.json`, `index-construction-dependency-lqc.json`, `index-construction-tests.log`, and `index-construction-fast.log`/TRX. Frozen harness: `artifacts/il-review/index-construction-final-bin/`.


### Dependency index caller-source obligation established

`DependencyIndexSource` now proves every direct call to the private index constructor comes from the identified resolver with the exact fresh resource stream. The receiver must be the current module's assembly and the name must be a literal or a closed integer-input character decoder behind a uniquely initialized delegate binding. Non-readonly bindings qualify only when all direct CIL uses establish a unique initializer assignment with no address exposure or later writes. Null delegate behavior and real type initialization remain on the unchanged runtime path.

The stream local has one assignment from the resource open. Every path to an index call must retain freshness: overwritten/address-exposed locals, earlier stream consumption or escape, repeated initialization of the same stream, alternate callers and exception paths without an established stream refuse the proof. Null tests are allowed. The resource identity is revalidated by unique name and exact hash.

Both Lqcuzgc and Uescfpvjk pass the caller-source proof against their respective validated index constructors. The integrated Lqcuzgc dependency-stage replay records this result and still captures the same protobuf-net bytes. `dependency.validatedIndexes` now includes `SourceClosed`. These are proof facts for a future checked transformation; no decoder, resource or emitted method was removed in this step. Table/record mutation ownership, initialization/locking preservation and decoder-effect closure remain before physical replacement.

Fourteen new cases cover literal and delegated names, null checks, wrong names, foreign framework calls, alternate callers, addresses, overwritten/consumed/reused streams, initialization bypasses, exception paths, mutable bindings and ambient decoder inputs. **33 focused tests pass**. The fast suite has **1,627 passes and the established 14 missing-sample failures**. Evidence: `artifacts/il-review/index-source-{lqc,uesc}.json`, `index-source-dependency-lqc.json`, `index-source-tests.log`, and `index-source-fast.log`/TRX. Frozen harness: `artifacts/il-review/index-source-final-bin/`.


### Dependency index mutation and publication obligations established

`DependencyIndexConsumers` now tracks table, record, key-collection, enumerator and out-record references through normal and exceptional CIL flow. Table reads, lookup/enumeration and private typed out-record calls are allowed; mutation, escape through aliases/calls/returns, field addresses, alternate record construction and reinitialization are refused. Recovered integer fields may only be written by construction. The identified resolver may still update its assembly/path caches. Exception-handler states include reference aliases from before later overwrites, and object-typed out addresses cannot silently lose record ownership.

Construction validation also captures each Hashtable publication. Every published record must already contain the same assigned integer fields as the final index, including overwritten keys and earlier table instances. A late offset rewrite now refuses construction even when two final snapshots agree. This closes the partially initialized-record gap; it does not authorize removing locking or cache behavior.

Both Lqcuzgc and Uescfpvjk pass construction (with publication checks), caller-source and consumer checks. The recovery stage saves `ConsumersClosed` alongside `SourceClosed` in `dependency.validatedIndexes`; its Lqcuzgc replay still recovers the exact protobuf-net payload. No emitted assembly is rewritten by these proofs. The remaining transformation obligations are byte-slice provenance at the decoder call, decoder effects/shared initialization, physical replacement/cleanup, resource ownership and the final full-corpus verification.

Twelve new consumer cases cover reads/cache writes, typed out entries, table mutation/escape, offset mutation/address exposure, foreign calls, entry alias escape, constructor reentry, object-typed out addresses and exception-handler aliases. One additional construction case rejects modification after publication. All **34 index-focused tests pass**. The fast suite has **1,640 passes and the established 14 missing-sample failures**. Evidence: `artifacts/il-review/index-consumers-{lqc,uesc}.json`, `index-consumers-dependency-lqc.json`, `index-consumers-focused.log`, and `index-consumers-fast.log`/TRX. Frozen harness: `artifacts/il-review/index-consumers-final-bin/`.


### Decoder input byte-slice provenance established

`DependencySliceSource` now follows the owned resource stream, its seek position, BinaryReader length/read operations, integer length decoding, buffer aliases and fresh inline MemoryStream arguments through every reaching control-flow path. It requires complete buffer reads and refuses mutation/escape/address exposure, missing reads, unknown offsets, changed resources, alternate decoder callers, repeated allocations and stored/reused input streams. Exception paths lose unsupported provenance. Decoder calls invalidate input-buffer knowledge until decoder effects are separately proved.

Both remaining dependency bundles pass the construction, caller-source, consumer and byte-slice checks. Lqcuzgc's decoder receives resource bytes at **offset 12, length 183,523**, SHA-256 `ef44fc916d69cf0cb052bb79e9c4e4a1dd7ea049746f41e8ced90837c7588ec8`. Uescfpvjk receives **offset 12, length 183,539**, SHA-256 `0894c271b75636169914b33aaf1f4230b08380633e0a7c4e8b7c1ba60caf31a4`. These results follow all direct calls and reaching paths, rather than relying only on sampled dependency requests. Unsupported paths refuse the proof.

The recovery stage saves these results in `dependency.validatedDecoderInputs`. Its Lqcuzgc replay retains the exact protobuf-net payload hash. No emitted assembly is changed by this step. Decoder-effect/shared-initialization closure is next, followed by physical replacement, helper/resource retirement and full-corpus validation.

Fourteen slice cases cover the valid slice and aliases, later source-stream seeks, partial/skipped reads, buffer mutations/escapes/addresses, stored streams, unknown offsets, alternate callers, changed resources, missing ownership and repeated allocations. All **55 focused dependency tests pass**. The fast suite has **1,654 passes and the established 14 missing-sample failures**. Evidence: `artifacts/il-review/slice-source-{lqc,uesc}.json`, `slice-source-dependency-lqc.json`, `slice-source-focused.log`, and `slice-source-fast.log`/TRX. ### Embedded dependency decoders physically retired (2026-10-13)

The preceding source, index-consumer and byte-slice proofs now support a committed transformation. `ClosedDependencyDecoder` requires two agreeing executions with a restricted intrinsic registry, immutable static inputs, unchanged caller-owned input bytes, closed object lifetimes and preserved shared initialization. `DependencyDecoderCompletionPass` revalidates construction and consumers, requires all direct callers to receive the same bytes and immediately consume the result through framework `MemoryStream.ToArray`, then substitutes a plain initialized-data helper. Repeated exact framework casts are accepted; alternate entries and other stream uses are refused. New types, methods and fields receive explicit structural-verification allowances; a failed verification rolls the mutation back. Cleanup still requires attribution and absence of surviving references.

Fresh runs from both intact original payloads succeed with physical removals:

| Payload | Removed protector declarations | Net emitted methods | Net emitted IL instructions | IL-verifier findings |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Lqcuzgc | 5 types, 49 methods | 559 → 511 | 20,643 → 18,432 | 34 → 29 |
| Uescfpvjk | 1 type, 23 methods | 561 → 539 | 20,697 → 19,992 | 34 → 31 |

Each replacement adds two small declarations for the helper/data layout and one method. Thus these are **72 removed methods, 70 fewer emitted methods, and 2,916 fewer emitted IL instructions** across the pair. Uescfpvjk's total type count rises by one because more of its original helper types still have retained members. Both original dependency decoder entry points and their crypto-selector switches disappear. All surviving switch methods, offsets and target counts remain unchanged; one Uescfpvjk method token moves during emission. All resource names, lengths and hashes are unchanged.

The emitted assemblies reload and pass structural verification. Replaying dependency recovery from each emitted assembly returns the exact same **288,256-byte protobuf-net assembly**, SHA-256 `b2a66e9864f81c2800a5afef4bfd1faf7c910e5a43ea2eb9dbb15c8ab6ffc633`. The replay requires **436,627 / 444,968 interpreted steps**, respectively, instead of hundreds of millions. This is an interpreter-work measurement, not a CLR timing claim. No surviving method acquires new or increased IL-verifier findings. Lqcuzgc's original-input comparison remains six newly reported methods, all previously unreadable, and zero worsened methods; its pinned expected total is now 29. The three remaining unreadable methods in each output are unchanged.

A metadata inventory of all **96 frozen emitted contexts / 76 distinct input hashes** finds this resolver shape in four contexts representing only these two payload identities. This scopes the targeted change; it does not replace the outstanding fresh full-corpus sweep. The fast suite has **1,672 passes and the established 14 missing-sample failures**. The focused dependency set contains **73 passing tests**, including real-CLR execution of a generated benign 288,256-byte data-holder fixture. No corpus assembly was CLR-executed.

**Remaining:** index construction still reads the original encrypted bundle, so neither bundle nor the unrelated retained protector resources is removed here. The replacement stores a second, plaintext copy of the dependency; some decompilers expand the initialized data into a large array literal. Retiring/replacing the proved bundle index and redundant reads, then using a readable plaintext resource representation, is the next dependency-completion step. Other retained helper members, type-verification defects and the final whole-corpus gates remain open. Full unprotection is not claimed.

### Redundant encrypted dependency input reads retired (2026-10-13)

`DependencyInputCompletionPass` now connects the decoder and index materialization proofs. It revalidates the exact generated index body, caller/source ownership and all input uses. A recovered helper must not read its argument. Read/seek results must be fixed across all reaching paths, and encrypted-buffer contents must not escape to an application consumer. The stronger discard analysis refuses runtime-type inspection or incompatible casts of the stream, and now rejects address exposure anywhere in the lifetime of a subsequently protected local, including before assignment.

The checked replacement removes the bundle open, the resolver's seek, `BinaryReader.ReadInt32` and byte-buffer read, plus the materialized index's position update. It retains a fresh non-null stream identity, buffer lengths/aliases and the resolver's selection, cache and loading paths. Exact source hashes are recorded as `dependency.retiredBundleReads` and passed through the existing resource-removal gates; attribution does not override remaining-access checks.

Serialized structural verification passes. Resource hashes, switch records and IL-verifier findings are unchanged: **28 / 30 findings** and the same three unreadable methods per output.

Before/after static resolver trials cover the exact assembly name, simple name, case-insensitive name with a different requested version, and an absent dependency. Each request is run cold and warm. Across both payloads, all **eight request scenarios** retain the same result kind and captured payload hash, successful requests capture the exact protobuf bytes once and reuse the cached assembly on the warm call, and missing requests return null without loading an assembly. This is bounded interpreter evidence, not a claim of CLR execution or exhaustive behavior comparison. The final stricter address-exposure proof accepts both saved pre-rewrite inputs.

The focused dependency/resource set has **128 passes**. The fast suite has **1,684 passes and the established 14 missing-sample failures**. New checks cover helpers that observe their input, stream type inspection, early local-address exposure, and exact-hash dependency resource attribution.

**Remaining:** the encrypted resources themselves are still present. Their original resolver input paths are gone, but the common resource gate still flags three reflection/access regions per payload: the application dynamic assembly/delegate invocation method, the resolver, and its assembly-name selector. The resolver also still evaluates now-unused resource-name/assembly expressions. ### Resource-access classification narrowed (2026-10-13)

Resource cleanup now recognizes two more closed metadata expressions: constructing an AssemblyName from a string and immediately reading FullName, and obtaining the current module's assembly from its own type only to cast/copy/pop the reference within one basic block. Framework scope, alternate entries and reference escape are checked. The original calls and exception behavior remain unchanged; these checks only classify whether the expression can expose a resource reader.

Read-only cleanup replays on the latest Lqcuzgc and Uescfpvjk outputs reduce the blocking methods from three to two each. The selector's assembly-name parser no longer blocks cleanup. The resolver's discarded assembly reference is classified, but its preserved Assembly.Load call still blocks it. The other blocker is application logic that loads runtime-supplied bytes and invokes an exported method. Removing or blanket-whitelisting that behavior would not establish safe resource deletion. Both encrypted bundle resources remain present, and this step changes no emitted assembly.

All 50 resource-cleanup tests pass, including nine new cases covering exact metadata parsing, casts, escape, foreign framework lookalikes and alternate entries. The fast suite reports 1,693 passes and the same 14 missing-sample failures. The application method was inspected statically; no corpus malware was executed. A fresh whole-corpus emission sweep remains outstanding.

### Recovered dependency array copy removed (2026-10-13)

After encrypted-input retirement, the generated dependency helper can now return its fresh initialized byte array directly. The checked transformation accepts only the generated initializer/array/RVA-data/MemoryStream body and direct callers that immediately cast the stream and call framework ToArray. It rejects escaping or stored streams, method pointers, alternate entries, changed helper bodies and empty buffers (whose original ToArray may return a shared empty array). Shared type initialization remains in order. Bodies and the helper signature roll back together if structural verification fails.

Fresh original-input runs for Lqcuzgc and Uescfpvjk commit this transformation. Each uncached dependency load avoids one MemoryStream allocation and one 288,256-byte array allocation/copy. ### Discarded stack copies and literal conversions removed across the emitted corpus (2026-10-13)

`DiscardedStackValues` replaces the integer-only carried-discard scan. Within each basic block it tracks literal values, safe duplicated stack copies, known-null local reads and nonthrowing integer conversions. A discarded duplicate does not authorize deleting the original value; subsequent rounds reconsider the producer only after that copy disappears. Unread reference stores qualify only for proved null values. Address-taken locals, incoming block values, checked conversions and effectful producers remain conservative. Persistent conversion histories keep tracking linear instead of copying increasingly long instruction lists.

A controlled replay covers all **96 recorded managed-output contexts**. **22 contexts improve, with 156 simplifications**, and all serialized outputs pass structural verification. ### Captured payload entry-point metadata modeled (2026-10-13)

The wrapper's next metadata gap is now covered: `Assembly.EntryPoint` reads the captured assembly bytes and copies only the entry-point reference/signature into detached metadata. It does not retain lazy metadata from a disposed image, borrow the home assembly's entry point, or load the payload into the CLR. A managed library with no entry point returns null; malformed metadata refuses. Parameter and return signatures use the existing reflection models. Invoking this captured entry point explicitly refuses cross-module dispatch rather than accidentally treating it as a home-module method. ### C# dynamic binder context distinguished from reflective member exposure

ModuleReachability now recognizes a bounded straight-line Type-handle flow solely into the lexical context parameter of framework Binder.Convert, UnaryOperation, BinaryOperation or GetIndex. Exact signatures/assembly owner/default calling convention are required; conversion-target positions, duplicated/escaped values, control-flow/EH entries and unrecognized consumers refuse. The binder's context is distinct from its dynamic receiver or conversion target (Microsoft documentation: https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/api/microsoft.csharp.runtimebinder.binder.convert?view=net-10.0). This is a generic compiler-pattern refinement, not sample-specific evidence or an allowance to ignore reflection.
